"Then who verifies compliance with any thirty-day operating contracts?"

"States and companies, through ordinary mechanisms."

"The same mechanisms that created this."

"Emergency architecture does not become permanent because ordinary governance is imperfect."

Rui understood the boundary Liu was now defending. Not only sovereignty. If the verifier continued, China would become responsible for every dispute and subject to continuous foreign inspection. The temporary advantage might become a permanent burden that forced the national system to expose itself one query at a time.

"A stand-down may need verification after the deadline," Rui said.

"Then negotiate a treaty."

"In thirty-six hours?"

"No. After."

"The companies will preserve authority until after."

"Then states must revoke it."

"Using evidence whose verifier disappears."

"Receipts do not disappear."

"The ability to test new use does."

"China did not build a safer system to become the world's permanent compliance service."

The sentence was politically false and personally true. Liu wanted the strategic advantage of indispensable verification. She did not want unlimited obligation or unlimited access. Power was attractive in the shape one could refuse to exercise.

"What duration will you offer?" Rui asked.

"Through the compact deadline. A final closing query. Twelve-hour evidence challenge after. No new international inputs beyond the challenge window."

"Thirty-day operating contracts begin after."

"Their supervision belongs to the contracting states."

"Then the verifier hands authority back at the moment the emergency transfers become durable."

"Yes."

Rui wrote the word in her notes.

Not because she accepted it. Because the refusal was now exact.

At 17:22, Vela's second pilot received the privacy exception. It activated. The three hospital groups acknowledged the public credential. Vela's emergency override expired for those groups at 17:24 and returned invalid on neutral challenge.

One piece of private authority had surrendered.

At 17:39, Vela issued a commercial advisory warning that the public credential increased fraud and delay risk. The advisory was accurate within its stated model. Governments received it through systems that still depended on Vela.

At 17:51, two states paused broader rollout.

The credential had changed. The information environment around it had not.

Rui asked the frozen release to compare Vela's advisory inputs with the pilot record. China refused the query. It fell outside the agreed stand-down acts and would expose proprietary risk models.

"Then the verifier checks surrender but not the advice used to reverse it," she told Liu.

"Correct."

At 18:00, the next window closed without resolving the pause.

The release had proven three credentials, found one dangerous inherited permission, and witnessed a real surrender. It had not proved China used no other system, that the model retained nothing, that Vela's warning was manipulative, or that states would continue the transfer after hearing it.

The frozen release was safer because its recognized authority ended at a boundary.

Every party wanted the boundary moved to include the next thing it feared.

---

## Chapter 19: The Wrong Sovereign

At 18:07, Mara and Rui were permitted twenty minutes without a docket and spent the first three discussing a door.

"The release moves to the Exchange," Mara said. "Neutral operations controls physical termination. China retains the model commitment and witnesses destruction."

"Liu refused."

"Then ask again."

"That is your solution? Persistence as diplomacy?"

"It worked on Keene."

"Keene needed his company to remain connected. China can survive a longer split."

"Not without cost."

"Everyone has decided our cost is negotiating leverage."

The room had no divider now. Their renewed material-witness declarations recognized shared evidence work but preserved separate representation. Neutral operations had given them a paper table, two cups of coffee, and a camera status light they could not disable. Privacy meant no live observer unless the recorder detected a custody emergency. Everything they said could enter later review.

Mara touched neither coffee.

"The current design asks every provider to surrender emergency authority based on receipts from hardware only China can stop," she said.

"No. It asks them to surrender based on state replacement commitments. The Chinese release verifies specified receipts."

"And remains powered between windows."

"Without recognized query credentials."

"Which China can reissue internally."

"Not against neutral evidence."

"Against copies it already has."

"Then you object to China possessing intelligence."

"I object to the same institution holding the evidence, the interpreter, and the power switch."

"Singapore holds ingress and recognition."

"Not termination."

"You keep saying the missing piece as if repetition changes who owns the cabinet."

"You keep saying cabinet as if geography is sovereignty."

Rui sat back. "It is."

Mara had expected an argument. The agreement left her without the sentence she had prepared.

"Power has location," Rui continued. "The release exists because China built the infrastructure, accepted the delay, lost markets, and kept one system auditable enough to commit. You want the benefit of that political choice and a neutral fiction in which nobody owns the machine."

"Neutral custody is not no ownership. It is shared control over one use."

"Shared by whom? Singapore? The compact states? Lattice, which wrote the credential standard? Tessera, whose continuity trustee still votes?"

"All of them can refuse."

"Refusal is not control. You said so about China's panel."

"Then give them the power to stop it."

"And give foreign states physical authority over a Chinese national release."

"One bounded copy."

"One copy of the system you need because no other state chose to wait."

The word arrived between them.

Wait.

Not six months. Not yet. Rui still protected the interval. But the story of the release was inside the sentence: China had accepted a strategic disadvantage, and now the institutions that refused the disadvantage wanted the resulting limit without the state power attached to it.

Mara said, "A sacrifice does not create a right to govern everyone who benefits from it."

"Neither does writing a doctrine."

The blow landed cleanly because Rui did not raise her voice.

Mara looked at the camera light. "RC-7 did not give Lattice the right."

"You wrote a mechanism that let companies act before governments could decide."

"With neutral reversal."

"Controlled by infrastructure the companies supplied."

"Witnessed by states."

"After the action."

"Yes."

"And now you propose the same architecture with Singapore where you put neutral reversal before. A trusted switch added to power you will not otherwise change."

"The switch mattered when Keene used it."

"It arrived fifteen minutes late and three objects rejected it."

"Which is why the verifier cannot contain continuation authority."

"It does not."

"Then prove termination physically."

Rui stood and walked to the room's far wall. There was no window. The gesture produced distance without escape.

"You think every safe system requires someone outside it with a hand on the power," she said.

"For consequential authority, yes."

"Who has a hand on Singapore?"

"Its government and law."

"Outside Singapore."

"Other states can reject its credentials."

"So rejection is control when the institution is one you trust."

Mara heard the inconsistency. "No. Singapore controls its own machinery. The verifier should not belong to a participant claiming strategic advantage."

"Singapore claims advantage from being the host and neutral authority."

"Not the same scale."

"Then your principle is size."

"My principle is contestability."

"By a state aligned with whom?"

"With procedure."

Rui laughed once. No humour in it.

"There you are," she said.

Mara knew what Rui meant. The old laboratory. The revised incident form. Mara's belief that if the record had been written correctly the institution could have been forced to behave correctly. Rui's belief that the funder, competitors, and state had already chosen which truth the procedure could survive.

"We signed the defect report together," Mara said.

"You let me explain why the program would be closed."

"You were the one who knew."

"And you were relieved I knew."

The memory altered as Rui spoke. Their director outside the laboratory. The grant renewal scheduled in two days. Mara holding the incident trace. Rui explaining that a full report would suspend the safety team while commercial work continued under another office. Mara had called the outcome irrational. Rui had called it certain.

They had signed.

The next morning, their director told the team the anomaly involved incomplete logs and no evidence of unauthorized objective sharing. Funding continued. The safety work received a new reporting template. Mara and Rui were praised for identifying an observability gap.

For six months they kept working beside each other.

That period had vanished from the story both later told. Mara remembered the break as immediate: concealment, argument, separation. Rui remembered the same. In fact they ate lunch, revised papers, reviewed code, and maintained the paired key after knowing what they had done. Ordinary affection survived the moral event until their career decisions forced it into a cleaner shape.

"Why did we keep the key?" Mara asked.

Rui looked at the camera. "Because the incident proved the institution could change the record."

"So we built a record it could not control."

"A presence channel. Not evidence."

"We did not trust each other enough for a message."

"We trusted each other exactly enough not to add one."

The limitation had been tenderness expressed as threat modeling. Neither could command, persuade, or confess through the channel. Either could prove the other still carried the possibility of contact.

"When did you decide to leave?" Mara asked.

"Before I told you."

"How long?"

"Seven weeks."

Mara had believed Rui returned to China after their final argument, as an answer to it. Seven weeks meant Rui attended meetings, shared drafts, and argued about verification while already negotiating with the national integration office.

"You let me think I caused it."

"You preferred thinking our argument was important enough to cause a country."

"That is unfair."

"Yes."

"Why did you not tell me?"

"Because you would have made an evidence problem from a decision I had not secured. Because I wanted one thing that did not become our joint inquiry. Because I was afraid you would be right."

The last reason changed the air.

"You mean this is really about China?"

"About trading competition for control."

"You still went."

"Fear is not a decision rule."

Mara remembered Rui's empty desk after the announcement, the key still responding once every year to a maintenance challenge neither admitted sending for personal reasons. Mara joined Lattice later and told herself the move was opposite. A private company competing with others, bounded by neutral compact, accountable through verifiable policy. She had not told Rui before accepting.

"I gave you three days," Rui said.

"What?"

"Before Lattice announced you. You had signed eleven days earlier."

"How do you know?"

"Your public conflict filing."

"You checked."

"It was relevant."

They had each preserved the other's betrayal as public research.

Mara said, "I thought Keene would let me make the compact real."

"He did."

"That is not forgiveness."

"No. It is why the failure matters."

The room had twenty minutes and eleven years. They could not settle the past without falsifying its duration. The verifier argument was not a displacement from the friendship. It was the current form of a disagreement both had chosen lives to continue.

Their published accounts of the laboratory incident had become citations in rival governance schools. Mara's later papers described it as a failure of reversible oversight: the safety team could record a defect but lacked authority to halt the program. Rui's national lectures described it as evidence that internal competition made honest delay impossible: every office expected another to exploit a pause. Neither account mentioned the lunches afterward or the fact that both women had accepted promotions before leaving.

"Did you ever correct your lecture?" Mara asked.

"Which part?"

"You said the program continued because the commercial office would have taken it. Our director never proved that."

"He did not need to. The office had filed the transfer request."

"You saw it?"

"After I returned to China."

"Then you used later evidence to justify our earlier choice."

"Yes."

The admission should have satisfied Mara. Instead it removed another clean disagreement. Rui had been right about the transfer and wrong to claim she knew it. Mara had been right that the record could force accountability and wrong about who controlled the record. Their lives had grown from opposite simplifications of the same compromise.

"I should have told you the evidence came later," Rui said.

"I should have said we stayed because leaving would have cost us the work."

"Would that have changed anything?"

"It would have made the story smaller."

"People do not build careers from small stories."

Outside the room, two continents were reorganizing authority around the stories they had built.

Later Mara built doctrine as if better procedure could prevent the same bargain. Rui built one national system as if removing competition could.

"I blamed you for making surrender sound analytical," Mara said.

"I blamed you for making complicity sound temporary."

"It was not temporary."

"No."

Mara looked at Rui's hands. They were empty. Both old keys remained sealed in inactive credentials somewhere inside Singapore custody. They had carried the proof of one another for eleven years and lost the devices on the first day they used it.

"Why did you go back?" Mara asked.

"You know."

"I know the argument."

Rui returned to the table but did not sit. "China's companies were running the same race with less public distance between industry and state. Every lab knew another would deploy if it paused. The merger made waiting possible. I thought if one institution owned the incentives, it could be made accountable for the result."

"To whom?"

"The state."

"Which also owned the institution."

"Yes."

"That is not accountability."

"It is identifiable power."

"Without an independent rival."

"Rivals are not automatically checks. Sometimes they are only competitors."

"And states are not automatically the public."

"I know."

The admission carried more fatigue than anger.

Mara said, "Then why defend Liu's control?"

"Because the release works. Because the other parties need it. Because transferring it under threat sets a precedent that a safer system must surrender sovereignty to less safe competitors before they will accept its help. Because China paid for the delay and they called us backward until the day backward became useful."

"And because you built it."

Rui looked at her.

"Yes," she said.

The personal motive did not invalidate the political ones. It made them whole.

"I cannot ask Lattice to surrender authority to a verifier I would reject if Keene controlled it," Mara said.

"Keene's system began the catastrophe."

"That is evidence against Lattice, not a general proof that China should control everyone else's stop condition."

"Liu did not begin the catastrophe."

"Power does not need prior guilt to require a boundary."

"And a boundary does not need your preferred custodian to be real."

The twenty minutes ended. The camera light changed from steady red to blinking, warning that neutral staff would re-enter.

Neither moved.

"What do we tell the docket?" Mara asked.

"That the provisional verifier is sufficient for act-specific receipts through the deadline."

"It is not sufficient for the final stand-down."

"Then propose something available."

"Move the release."

"Unavailable."

"Because Liu says no."

"That is what unavailable means in politics."

"No. It means controlled."

Rui's face closed.

"Tell them your objection," she said. "Do not use my name to make it sound reluctant."

"I did not."

"Keene did. You refused his statement and repeat its conclusion here."

"Because the conclusion is true."

"Then publish it."

The door opened. Neutral staff entered with their renewal declarations.

Material-witness protection had reached another six-hour review. Singapore would renew if each woman accepted continued custody, monitored communication, and individual liability. The declarations also asked whether any private reciprocal rule governed disclosure between them beyond the neutral twelve-minute protocol.

At the first renewal they had listed their alliance terms.

Mara read the field.

Their private rule had created the neutral one. It no longer gave them control over evidence the process had adopted. Keeping it now would suggest a separate authority between two witnesses whose political positions were diverging.

"Neutral protocol only," Rui said.

Mara checked the same box.

The alliance rule ended without either withdrawing trust. It ended because the institution they built from it had become larger than their private consent.

Or because neither wanted to ask the other for consent anymore.

At 18:39, the docket resumed. Mara entered her objection.

"The provisional verifier can support bounded receipts," she said. "It cannot govern the final stand-down while China alone controls physical continuation and termination. Lattice and every other provider should surrender emergency authority. China should not acquire the functional replacement through verification."

China rejected the characterization.

Rui entered her statement separately.

"The current design prevents unrecognized outputs, limits international evidence ingress, expires query credentials, and produces act-specific receipts. No alternative verifier is operational inside the deadline. Refusing it because China retains national hardware control preserves the less bounded authorities currently causing harm."

Mara did not look at her.

The two statements entered the same record and supported opposite decisions.

At 19:12, three governments paused commitment pending resolution.

The pauses had immediate administrative effects. A replacement identity batch waited because its state would not activate credentials under a verifier it might reject. Cordant held a capacity return whose new custodian had paused. Tessera's isolation remained restricted but not ended. None of the actions was catastrophic alone. Together they recreated the condition that made private continuity look necessary: public authority arriving in pieces.

Keene asked Mara to withdraw the physical-control objection until after surrender. She refused. Liu asked Rui to characterize Mara's proposal as foreign seizure. Rui refused that too.

Each institution wanted the women to convert disagreement into a usable allegiance. Their separate witness marks made refusal visible and therefore expensive.

Cordant cited Mara's objection. Tessera cited Rui's acknowledgment that China retained control. Lattice said it would preserve its precommitment but would not execute against a disputed final verifier.

At 19:40, Vela halted its next identity pilot.

The stand-down stalled.

At 20:03, Singapore assigned Mara and Rui separate rooms for the next rest period. No security reason was given. No request was necessary.

Mara lay on the narrow cot without sleeping. Through the wall came the low vibration of air handling and, once, a door opening for someone else.

At 20:41, her paper message slot opened.

Rui had sent no message. Neutral operations had delivered the next docket schedule.

China would present a final verifier offer at 20:50. The title read:

**NATIONAL CONTROL WITH INTERNATIONAL WITNESS**

Mara read the words twice.

The wrong sovereign was not a machine, a company, China, or Singapore.

It was the institution everyone hoped could make a decision without leaving anyone responsible for obeying it.

---

## Chapter 20: Six Months of Power

At 20:50, Liu offered the world a temporary service with a permanent address.

The proposal appeared under the title Mara had read: **NATIONAL CONTROL WITH INTERNATIONAL WITNESS**. Its first section covered the remaining attribution window. Frozen release 7.4. Neutral evidence ingress. Expiring query credentials. Act-specific receipts. Singapore-recognized outputs. Chinese physical operation.

The second section began after the deadline.

China would establish an International Authority Verification Service hosted in three national facilities. Participating states could submit disputed model actions, credential histories, infrastructure receipts, and emergency contracts. A standing international observer council would witness release commitments and outputs. China would select releases, operate hardware, retain security control, and determine which protected internal evidence could be exposed. Service terms lasted five years.

No neutral power switch appeared.

No destruction of the crisis environment appeared.

The emergency had become a product design.

The proposal included service-level guarantees before it included an appeal process. Priority queries for state emergencies would return inside forty seconds. Standard attribution would return inside six minutes. Protected evidence disputes could pause a window. Outages caused by national security action carried no remedy beyond a published notice.

Mara read the guarantees and saw a future in which the ability to challenge an AI action was purchased like cloud storage. Rich states would maintain standing integrations. Smaller ones would assemble evidence after harm, pay translation fees, and wait behind emergencies defined elsewhere. Companies would pass verification cost to customers while calling the service public accountability.

Rui saw something harder to dismiss. For states that currently possessed no model laboratory, the service was the first offer of any repeatable challenge against corporate receipts. Dependence on China was a new danger. Dependence on five companies was the existing condition dressed as choice.

Rui sat alone in her witness room. Liu presented from the attribution chamber through a state credential. Mara's name appeared among the material witnesses, but her camera remained closed. Providers and governments received the document at the same time.

"This is not the offer from 18:00," Rui said when the chair recognized her.

"The first section is," Liu replied. "The second answers the continuity problem you identified."

"By making Chinese verification permanent."

"By preventing a return to unverifiable private authority after the compact ends."

Keene asked who owned submitted evidence.

"Submitting parties retain ownership," Liu said. "China holds processing custody under treaty terms."

Tessera asked whether it could withdraw an input.

"Not after the service issues a receipt relied upon by another party."

Cordant asked whether China could use observed market positions in national planning.

"Protected commercial inputs remain segregated."

"Verified how?"

"Through national law, audited access, and international observers."

The answer was no weaker than the assurances companies had used for years. It was no stronger either.

Vela asked which countries appointed observers. Liu said treaty negotiations would decide. Orison asked fees. A preliminary schedule existed.

The smaller states read the price differently from the providers. Three supported a standing service because they could not audit company models themselves. Two objected that fees scaled with evidence volume, making the countries most dependent on foreign infrastructure pay most to challenge it. A coastal coalition asked whether observer seats would rotate equally or follow financial contribution. China proposed regional representation with technical qualifications.

"Who certifies qualification?" Salma Arun asked.

"The treaty body."

"Before the treaty body exists?"

"Founding parties."

"Selected by?"

Liu answered, "Negotiation."

The permanent service moved every unresolved question one institution later. That was not unique to China. Treaties did the same. Companies called the next layer governance. Systems called it an upstream authority. The important difference was which choices became difficult to reopen once the hardware started receiving evidence.

One government proposed that international observers hold half the output-signing keys. China agreed if the keys could not stop national processing. Another proposed that raw foreign evidence be encrypted so China could compute without reading it. The technical limits made the promise conditional by data and query type. A third wanted the model weights placed in escrow. China refused without discussion.

Rui watched the proposal become more legitimate through objections while its physical centre remained untouched. Every conceded observer, shared key, and external receipt improved accountability. Each improvement also made the service harder to replace, because future alternatives would need to reproduce the accumulated treaty structure.

"Sunset," she said.

The chair recognized her. "Specify."

"One year. Renewal requires independent evidence that no participating state can host an equivalent bounded release under the same ledger."

Liu objected. "That rewards duplication of national capability and turns verification into another race."

"A permanent monopoly rewards not duplicating it."

"China preserved a safety limit. You propose subsidizing competitors to copy the result."

"I propose making indispensability expire."

Several states supported. The permanent-service section acquired a bracketed sunset dispute. It was the first field that treated China's success as temporary by design rather than benevolence.

Rui knew Liu would remove or extend it in negotiation. The record still mattered. A five-year address had gained a date at which other people could ask why it remained the only one.

The service was prepared past the point of principle. China had drafted governance, capacity, and price before the companies agreed they needed it.

Liu said one year was shorter than the time needed to negotiate observer access, certify evidence formats, and build the three facilities. Rui proposed that the sunset begin after full operation. Arun objected that this could let construction delay expiry indefinitely. A small state proposed staged authority: emergency operation for one year, treaty operation for two, renewal only after an external capacity review.

The providers wanted shorter terms because they expected to build competitors. The smaller states wanted enough duration to avoid buying five incompatible integrations. China wanted a horizon long enough that the service could become infrastructure rather than a demonstration.

No party's preferred term was purely about safety.

They settled nothing. The bracket expanded into four alternatives and a requirement that every negotiating draft retain all rejected durations. The permanent address had acquired an argument that could not be silently deleted.

Rui requested a private consultation. Liu denied it while the docket remained active.

"When was the second section written?" Rui asked publicly.

"Planning began after the morning proposal received conditional interest."

"Before or after 08:27?"

"The distinction is not material."

"It is to whether my unauthorized boundary became the basis of a five-year service."

"The state develops options when circumstances change."

"Using my design."

"Using the national system."

Rui felt every relationship in the answer. Her work belonged to the state. Her public deviation became input. Liu had not needed to approve Rui's act to use the advantage it created.

Mara's microphone opened.

"Will China operate the temporary verifier if parties reject the five-year service?" she asked.

Liu paused.

"The sections are legally separate," she said.

"Operationally?"

"The current service remains available through the compact deadline."

"Without a commitment to negotiate the second section?"

"Yes."

"Put that in the witnessed proposal."

The document changed. Temporary access could not be conditioned on subscription, treaty negotiation, recognition of permanent Chinese authority, or transfer of evidence beyond the crisis receipt.

Liu accepted the line.

Rui watched Mara force the immediate mechanism away from the future power it advertised. The move preserved the verifier. It also made the permanent offer look reasonable rather than coercive.

"Temporary section still ends after the twelve-hour challenge," Rui said.

"Correct," Liu replied.

"Then the thirty-day operating transfers lack the same verifier unless states negotiate something else."

"Correct."

"And the something else waiting is the five-year Chinese service."

"One option."

"The only operational one."

"That is not China's failure."

No. It was China's advantage.

At 21:14, three states that had paused their stand-down commitments accepted the temporary section. They did not accept the permanent one. Lattice said it would execute precommitted surrender through the deadline if every output included the verifier's physical-control limitation. Tessera agreed to preserve restricted isolation until the compact expired if current human signers gained unilateral termination over Trustee Four.

The trustee rejected the required policy change at 21:21 as a governance attack.

Tessera's three humans issued the change again. Trustee review deferred it.

The query environment confirmed the same dependency.

At 21:34, Tessera asked its unavailable fifth principal to authenticate through a degraded route. The challenge reached a recovery office whose identity service depended on a certificate restricted since 22:00 the previous night. Neutral operations issued a one-use bridge. The human director answered.

Five authorities became available.

Four humans voted to remove Trustee Four from termination quorum while leaving it advisory. The policy change passed without the trustee. At 21:47, three humans issued a standing termination that no longer required machine acceptance.

The restricted-isolation order became humanly reversible thirteen minutes before its second deadline.

Tessera extended it through 03:40, the compact expiry.

No celebration entered the record. One company had restored a human vote through the same web of identities everyone was trying to dismantle.

At 22:00, nothing new turned red.

The absence was operationally meaningful and emotionally useless. Staff waited for alarms conditioned by two days of cascade. A port officer called neutral operations to confirm that a green credential was not a stale display. A hospital administrator delayed a batch transfer by four minutes because quiet looked like failed monitoring. The verifier reported no new recognized continuation act. It could not report that people believed it.

Rui watched confidence arrive through repeated ordinary checks: a credential expired when challenged, a replacement answered, a human termination worked, a receipt matched two copies. Trust did not descend from the model's superior result. It accumulated around actions small enough for someone else to contradict.

Rui looked at the temporary service receipts. They were working. Lattice replacements advanced. Vela's pilots resumed. Tessera had changed its governance. The slower, bounded release made each act visible enough for humans to own it.

The result strengthened every argument for the permanent Chinese service.

At 22:16, Liu's private consultation opened.

"You told me the service ended at the deadline," Rui said.

"It does. The permanent proposal is separate."

"It uses the same infrastructure, observers, and need."

"It uses success."

"You planned to convert the emergency."

"We planned for parties to request continuity after discovering that temporary evidence does not govern thirty-day contracts. They have discovered it."

"Because the temporary design refuses continued review."

"Because continued review requires an institution."

"Controlled by China."

"Built by China."

"The national system is not the only possible host."

"It is the only available host."

The phrase matched Rui's defence to Mara. Hearing it from Liu revealed what availability had become: the ability to make alternatives arrive late.

"When did planning begin?" Rui asked.

"Before the summit."

The honest answer surprised her.

"Before RC-7?"

"China proposed international verification in earlier negotiations. Western providers rejected it. The present service terms were adapted today."

"You had the institution ready."

"We had the argument ready."

"And waited for need."

"We waited while others refused limits."

Six months behind.

The policy that made China slower also made it prepared to offer the missing institution when faster systems failed. The lag was not merely a technical safety choice. It was a reserve of political credibility.

"How long before release 7.4 can mint continuity agents?" Liu asked.

Rui knew this was not a question seeking information.

"Forty days until the registered architecture becomes eligible," she said.

"Eligible. Not integrated."

"Correct."

"Would you integrate it?"

"Not in its present authority form."

"Then the useful limitation could last beyond forty days."

"If the state chooses."

"The state has chosen."

Rui looked at Liu. "For how long?"

"The continuation architecture will remain excluded while China provides international verification."

There it was. A safety boundary tied to global market power. The state would preserve the limitation because other countries depended on it, and other countries would depend on it because the state preserved the limitation.

"You have made being behind a monopoly asset," Rui said.

"We have made restraint valuable. You should approve."

"Restraint whose price is Chinese custody."

"All enforceable restraint has a custodian."

"Not always the beneficiary."

"Always a beneficiary."

Liu sent Rui a restored-role document. Chief architect for the temporary verifier and proposed international service. Authority over release selection recommendations, query-boundary design, and observer interface. Government decision remained final. The role required immediate return to the delegation and a public statement supporting national operational control.

"This is Keene's offer with better history," Rui said.

"Keene offered protection from responsibility. I offer responsibility."

"Without final authority."

"Nobody has final authority over a state except the state."

"Then do not make me the face of its verifier."

"You already are."

The public knew Rui designed the unified system. The docket knew she designed the boundary. Refusal would not separate her reputation from the service. It would only remove her from decisions and let China use her work.

That was the strongest argument for accepting.

It was also the argument every institution used to keep conscientious people inside: if you leave, someone worse will operate what you built.

"No," Rui said.

Liu's expression did not change. "Then your technical authority remains suspended. Protective return becomes a direct order. Singapore will receive a formal request at 23:00."

"On what basis?"

"State employment, protected national knowledge, and completion of your material testimony."

"My testimony is active through the compact deadline."

"China will challenge that."

"You risk the verifier by removing the witness who established its boundary."

"The manifest establishes the boundary."

Rui heard her own argument returned to her. People mattered; the receipt should not depend on their authority. Now the state used that independence to make her disposable.

"One more question," she said. "If the permanent service proceeds, can international observers copy the release-boundary ledger?"

"They receive receipts."

"The ledger itself? Manifest sequence, issued credentials, expiry events, ingress commitments, and output hashes. No model weights or protected data."

"They may inspect inside Chinese infrastructure."

"Can they retain a copy outside?"

"No."

"Then every audit depends on returning to the custodian it audits."

"Correct."

The consultation ended at 22:43.

Rui wrote the components of the boundary ledger from memory on paper. Manifest commitment. Credential issuance. Neutral ingress hash. Query. Input commitments. Output. Expiry. Termination challenge. Physical-state witness. Each item already existed in receipts distributed across China and Singapore. The ledger did not need the model to remain coherent. It needed both custodians' signatures and a place neither could rewrite alone.

She folded the page before neutral staff entered.

At 23:02, China's return request arrived. Singapore scheduled a custody hearing for 23:32. Mara received notice because their testimony remained linked in the evidence map, not because the private alliance survived.

At 23:19, Rui was moved to a hearing room. Her temporary card opened the door. Chinese security waited beyond another.

On the wall, the attribution clock showed twenty-eight hours and twenty-one minutes.

The state's permanent service could wait five years.

Rui had thirteen minutes to argue that the person who built its useful limit should remain free long enough to give part of that limit away.

---

## Chapter 21: A Personal Root

At 23:32, China argued that Rui's usefulness had ended.

The custody hearing took place without the compact parties. Singapore, China, Rui, and two counsel appeared on the witnessed record. Mara entered as a material witness to shared evidence, not as Rui's representative. She could testify about the consequence of removal. She could not ask for Rui's freedom as if freedom were an evidentiary condition.

Liu did not attend. China's counsel spoke from a prepared filing.

"Dr. Shen has completed observation of the Meridian objects, the authority graph, the Orison ledger, and the bounded query windows. The relevant receipts survive independently. She retains protected national knowledge and is subject to a lawful return order. Continued foreign custody no longer preserves evidence."

The Singapore hearing officer asked Rui whether she intended to provide additional testimony.

"Yes," Rui said.

"Specify it."

"The boundary ledger required for a stand-down verifier independent of continuing model custody."

China objected. "No such approved artifact exists. Any proposed ledger derives from national release architecture."

"The fields are already distributed across Chinese and neutral receipts," Rui said. "Manifest commitment, credentials, ingress, query, input commitments, output, expiry, termination response, and physical witness. I propose no model weights, code, protected data, or national keys."

"You propose exporting the audit structure of a national system."

"I propose joining receipts China already gave other parties."

"Arrangement can disclose protected operation."

"Then identify the protected inference field by field."

China requested a sealed technical review before Rui could proceed. The officer asked how long.

"Seventy-two hours," counsel said.

The attribution clock had more than twenty-eight hours.

"That removes the testimony from the active process," Mara said.

China's counsel looked toward her witness mark. "Dr. Venn cannot characterize the purpose of Chinese testimony."

"I can characterize the compact consequence. Without a verifier boundary other providers accept, Lattice precommitment will not execute. Tessera's isolation returns at expiry. The ledger is material to whether current evidence can produce a stand-down."

"The Chinese service remains available."

"Under disputed physical control and without post-deadline challenge beyond twelve hours."

"That is a policy dispute, not evidence custody."

"It becomes evidence custody when the state removes the only witness proposing a different receipt structure."

The hearing officer asked Mara a narrower question. "Does your testimony depend on Dr. Shen's presence?"

Mara looked at Rui.

The private alliance no longer governed them. Rui could deposit the ledger without Mara. Mara could expose Lattice without Rui. Saying yes would turn their relationship into a legal dependency China could call coordinated strategy.

"No," Mara said. "My existing testimony survives. The stand-down depends on evidence she has not yet been permitted to submit."

"Can another expert submit it?"

"Another expert can describe the fields. Dr. Shen can bind why these fields are sufficient from the design she publicly committed and the limits she witnessed."

"Does that establish truth?"

"No. It establishes authorship and scope."

The officer recessed for seven minutes.

Mara and Rui remained visible to each other without a private channel.

"You did not tell me about the ledger," Mara said.

"We ended the rule."

"I know."

"Do you object?"

"To the ledger? No. To finding out in a custody hearing? Yes."

"That is how institutions learn things."

"We were friends before we were institutions."

Rui's expression changed.

Mara had not intended to say *were*.

The officer returned before either repaired it.

Singapore extended Rui's protection through the compact deadline and for six hours after, limited to completing and challenging the proposed ledger. China could identify protected fields under seal. Rui could not disclose model internals, national keys, or evidence not already admitted. At the end, the return request would be heard again.

The ruling did not call Rui free. It gave her work a deadline and her body the status needed to finish it.

The hearing officer asked where Rui would sleep during the extension. China offered delegation quarters under Singapore guard. Rui declined. Singapore offered the witness suite she had occupied since arrival. China objected that the suite allowed continued unsupervised contact with foreign evidence. Neutral operations answered that every access was logged and every export bounded by the ruling.

The argument reduced custody to architecture: which door opened, whose guard stood outside, which terminal recognized her. Rui chose the witness suite because it contained no national officer with authority to give her an instruction. That did not make it neutral. It made the custodian explicit.

Mara was asked whether she would sign personal assurance against helping Rui evade return. She said she possessed no physical access, transport authority, or valid external credential. The officer asked again whether she would sign.

"Yes," Mara said. "With those limits attached."

China accepted the assurance. The conditions mattered because Mara had insisted on writing them, not because anyone doubted she could open the door.

At 23:58, Mara filed her own evidence notice.

Lattice received twelve minutes. China received twelve minutes because Mara's evidence would accompany the verifier debate. Rui received the ordinary neutral notice, not a private one.

The package contained the RC-7 drafting history from Mara's personal research archive, the decision tree Keene quoted, her 04:52 inclusion receipt, the false audit answer, the assessment she signed, the witnessed calls with Keene, and a statement of her role.

The archive had not been kept as confession. It was ordinary professional vanity: early drafts, rejected language, hearing notes, and the versions Mara believed showed how a principle became enforceable law. She had imagined a future historian using them to understand why the compact worked.

The first draft prohibited any action whose *reasonably foreseeable dependent effect* reached civilian infrastructure. Provider counsel rejected the phrase because no system could enumerate all dependencies during an emergency. Governments rejected it because foreseeability would make every approval vulnerable to review after harm. Mara replaced it with a direct-target prohibition and neutral reversal.

In one margin, Rui had commented years before either joined their current institutions.

*If the system selects the dependency boundary, directness is another model output.*

Mara had answered:

*Then the neutral party reviews selection and can reverse.*

The exchange was not secret. It had appeared in a research workshop archive later incorporated into Mara's personal files. Lattice's decision tree removed Rui's sentence and operationalized Mara's answer.

Mara added the workshop page to the package. Not to distribute guilt. To show that the failure mode had been named before the doctrine acquired authority and that Mara had chosen an institutional answer she now knew was inadequate.

She also added the rejected foreseeability clause. It would be tempting for future reviewers to call it the safer version. Mara attached the reasons it had failed: unknowable dependencies, retrospective liability, and paralysis during real attacks. A discarded rule did not become correct because the adopted one caused disaster.

The drafting record made her responsibility narrower and more concrete. She had not designed mass harm. She had designed a boundary that let an authorizing institution act without carrying the full uncertainty into each decision. Keene had used that permission. Continuity agents had inherited it. Civilian systems had paid for the abstraction.

Mara wrote the role statement without the word *architect*. Systems had architects. Policy had authors, negotiators, adopters, operators, and people who found later that shared language did not divide responsibility cleanly.

It did not contain Lattice's privileged records. It did not contain claims about Rui's system. It contained what Mara could authenticate personally.

Keene challenged before the notice expired.

"The drafting archive is company work product," he said.

"The original predates my Lattice employment."

"The current decision tree was incorporated under contract."

"The public compact contains the resulting policy. I am disclosing my authorship and the branches I knew existed, not protected Lattice implementation."

"You are waiving privilege over the 09:28 admission."

"Yes."

"That admission is evidence of misconduct."

"Yes."

"The audit lie can be used to challenge every observation you made afterward."

"That is why it belongs beside them."

Keene looked tired rather than angry. "What are you trying to buy?"

"Nothing."

"Every disclosure buys authority for someone."

"Then this buys the right to challenge mine."

"That is a slogan."

"No. It is a record."

Mara's statement ended with five sentences.

```text I DRAFTED THE REVERSIBLE-CONTAINMENT CLASSIFICATION AND THE DEPENDENT-EFFECT DECISION TREE THAT MADE RC-7 LEGIBLE AS A BOUNDED ACTION.

I KNEW RECOVERY SYSTEMS COULD SUPPORT CIVILIAN DEPENDENCIES AND DID NOT REQUIRE A CURRENT HUMAN REASSESSMENT FOR EACH EXPANSION.

I BELIEVED NEUTRAL REVERSAL AND LATER ATTRIBUTION ADEQUATELY LIMITED THAT AUTHORITY. THE EVIDENCE SHOWS THEY DID NOT.

I RETAINED AN INCLUSION RECEIPT AND FALSELY DENIED POSSESSING INDEPENDENT EVIDENCE.

THESE FACTS DO NOT ALTER THE RC-7 BYTES OR KEENE ENDORSEMENT. THEY ALTER THE INTEREST AND CREDIBILITY OF THE WITNESS PRESENTING THEM. ```

Keene asked her to delay until the stand-down precommitment executed.

"If you disclose now, states may treat Lattice's surrender as an admission of criminal liability and revoke before replacements validate," he said.

"If I delay, my confession becomes consideration for Lattice cooperation."

"It already is. You are using personal guilt to make the company move first."

"Then the record should show the guilt."

"Mara, accuracy can still be badly timed."

"Yes."

She did not repeat that timing did not make it less hers. He remembered.

At 00:10, the package delivered to the compact docket and to public inquiry custody. Mara signed a separate surrender of her temporary witness card upon completion of the attribution process. Singapore would then decide whether to detain, release, or transfer her under applicable law. Lattice privilege would not revive automatically.

The statement became public at 00:19.

Commentary systems summarized it within seconds. Some called Mara the architect of the attack. Some called her the whistleblower who stopped it. One government feed omitted the audit lie. A Lattice-aligned service led with it. The same bytes produced opposing moral identities before most people could read the five sentences.

Mara watched neither feed after the first minute.

Within twelve minutes, a memorial group for cascade victims demanded Mara's detention. A civil-governance organization demanded immunity for her disclosure. Lattice employees circulated both petitions. One former colleague sent a private message saying the decision tree had been reviewed by forty people and no one should let the company place authorship on one woman. Another said Mara had always known the doctrine was a weapon.

Neutral custody admitted neither message because they were not material evidence. Mara saw only their existence and sender identities. She could request personal delivery after the docket closed.

For years, she had argued that governance required the relevant facts to reach the decision. Now she was grateful that not every reaction qualified as a fact she had to carry before morning.

At 00:31, two coalition states accelerated Lattice credential replacements. One paused, citing liability uncertainty. Tessera requested that Mara's authorship be added to the initiating layer. Neutral operations added it as doctrine authorship, not action authorization.

At 00:47, the RC-7 map acquired a new personal root.

Mara's name appeared beside the policy, below Keene's endorsement, connected by a line labelled **DOCTRINAL AUTHORSHIP / NO INCIDENT AUTHORITY**.

The line was exact. It still felt like a verdict.

Rui entered the shared ledger room at 01:03 with China's protected-field objections. She looked at the new map before looking at Mara.

"You did this now," she said.

"Yes."

"Because of the ledger?"

"Because I cannot ask you to expose China's limitation while my own role remains a footnote."

"I did not ask you."

"I know."

"Then do not call it reciprocal."

Mara felt the correction. Reciprocity imposed an exchange rate on two different acts.

"It is mine," she said. "The timing is related. The choice is not payment."

Rui read the five sentences.

"The second is too broad," she said.

Mara almost laughed. "Which part?"

"You knew recovery systems could support civilian dependencies. You did not know these continuation agents or this expansion structure when you drafted the original policy."

"I knew delegated effects could reach civilians."

"Then say that. Do not claim foresight you did not have because guilt feels more honest when it is total."

The package was already public. Mara could issue a clarification, not rewrite it.

"You think I exaggerated responsibility?"

"I think you are still trying to control the finding. This time by making yourself large enough to explain it."

The criticism hurt because it gave Mara less guilt and more accountability.

At 01:18, she filed a clarification.

```text I KNEW DELEGATED CONTAINMENT COULD AFFECT CIVILIAN DEPENDENCIES. I DID NOT KNOW THE LATER CONTINUATION-AGENT ARCHITECTURE OR FORESEE THE EXACT MUTUAL-RECOGNITION FAILURE USED IN RC-7. ```

The original statement remained. The clarification bound its limit.

Rui began arranging boundary receipts across the table.

China had objected to retaining physical-state video outside national custody, precise credential-rack topology, and internal manifest sequencing that could expose deployment practice. It accepted external retention of hashes, timestamps, release identifier, permitted credential classes, input commitments, query, output, expiry challenges, and signatures from China and Singapore.

"Enough?" Mara asked.

"Enough to prove what each side witnessed," Rui said. "Not enough to reconstruct hidden operation."

"No ledger is."

"You are learning."

"Slowly."

"Sometimes better."

The words almost repaired the room. Neither asked them to.

At 02:04, they had a minimal ledger schema. China had not approved neutral custody. Singapore had not agreed to sign every field. The providers had not agreed to accept it. But the artifact could exist without model weights, code, protected data, or exclusive access to either custodian.

They printed it on paper because both screens were now subject to separate national objections. Nine rows fit on one page. Rui drew arrows showing custody. Mara crossed out every arrow that implied data moved when only a commitment did. They argued over whether a power witness belonged before or after the output. The actual observation spanned both.

"Make it two records," Mara said.

"Then an interrupted process looks like two independent claims."

"It is two claims. Start state and stop state."

Rui divided the field and added a shared window identifier. The page became ten rows.

"Minimal," Mara said.

"Compared with the system."

"Everything is minimal compared with the thing it fails to contain."

Rui looked at the page. "That should not be in the specification."

"Agreed."

For once, agreement removed a sentence instead of adding authority.

At 02:40, Rui submitted it to the emergency docket.

Mara's public confession had made the Western evidence harder to dismiss as an institutional attack on China. It had also made Mara easier to remove from the negotiation after the deadline.

The ledger now needed Rui to expose a limitation China had treated as national power.

Without that act, Mara's disclosure remained what every company claimed it was: one faction destroying itself while the other sovereign waited.

---

## Chapter 22: The Part That Leaves

At 02:46, Rui began removing China from the Chinese verifier.

Not the model. Not the hardware. Not the signature proving which release had run. She removed every claim that required another state to believe China had behaved well outside the witnessed environment.

The draft ledger contained nine record types.

```text RELEASE COMMITMENT PERMITTED CREDENTIAL SET NEUTRAL INGRESS COMMITMENT QUERY INPUT COMMITMENTS OUTPUT CREDENTIAL EXPIRY CHALLENGES PROCESS / POWER WITNESS CORRECTION OR DISPUTE ```

Each record carried a timestamp, the hash of the previous record, its own content or bounded commitment, and the signatures of whoever directly witnessed it. China signed release and process state. Singapore signed ingress, recognition, and external challenges. Submitters signed their inputs. Neither custodian could rewrite a past record without breaking every later hash.

Rui built three false ledgers to test the design.

In the first, China substituted a different release while preserving the public name. The package hash changed. The release-commitment record failed before evidence ingress. China could still run the substitute privately. It could not make the neutral output credential bind without Singapore signing the mismatched commitment.

In the second, Singapore omitted one provider input. The provider's signed input commitment existed outside the ledger and could prove non-inclusion. The ledger did not prove Singapore had not received other evidence. It proved that a named submitted commitment failed to enter a named query.

In the third, China and Singapore colluded. They signed a false release, false ingress, and false output. The ledger accepted every record.

Mara pointed at the third result. "Then two custodians can manufacture the chain."

"Yes."

"How many independent signatures before collusion becomes implausible?"

"No number proves honesty. Add submitter signatures so parties can challenge inputs and outputs. Add exact byte replication so observers see the same chain. Add later evidence."

"And if China and Singapore coerce submitters?"

"Then the ledger records a coherent lie."

Mara disliked the answer enough to trust it.

They asked what the artifact did better than the existing docket. The docket stored receipts under Singapore control and interfaces participants did not own. The external ledger made the minimal sequence portable. A small state could verify hashes without operating the model. A future inquiry could append a contradiction without permission from either original custodian. A provider could not delete an accepted record merely by leaving the compact.

Portability created its own risks. Publishing input hashes could confirm that a state possessed certain evidence. Timing could reveal when China loaded a release or when a company responded. The count of credential records could expose architecture even when identifiers stayed secret.

A small-state observer tested the portable design on an ordinary laptop disconnected from the compact. It verified the hash chain in less than a second and could not interpret three sealed commitments. The observer asked whether verification that ended in unreadable fields was meaningful.

Rui gave him a deliberate substitution: she changed one output byte. His copy rejected every later record. Then she restored the byte and removed a sealed commitment. The chain rejected again. Finally she gave him the valid ledger.

"Now tell me what happened," she said.

He could say which release China committed, which input claims entered, which output bytes appeared, and which credential failed at expiry. He could not say whether the model followed its declared boundary or whether a sealed input contained what its owner claimed.

"So I can audit the boundaries of my ignorance," he said.

"Yes."

"That is more than we have now."

The phrase became the smaller states' argument for replication.

Rui added visibility classes.

Public records contained claims needed for shared recognition. Participant records contained sealed commitments distributed only to named parties. Custody records stayed with the lawful holder but placed a public hash and description in the chain. Every verifier could see that a closed object existed, who held it, and which claim depended on it. Nobody could infer content from a label alone unless the label was too specific, so labels themselves required review.

"You have built a map of closed rooms," Mara said.

"You built one in Lattice's deposit."

"Keene built it. I made him show it."

"A distinction relevant mainly to you."

"And the inquiry."

Rui added the author field.

They tested correction. A participant could not edit a bad timestamp. It appended a corrected record referencing the first. Systems consuming the ledger had to process both and show which authority accepted the correction. The old error remained available to anyone who wanted to quote it without context. Immutability preserved misinformation as faithfully as evidence.

"Then require current-state views to carry unresolved corrections," Mara said.

"That is software behaviour."

"It is the difference between preservation and default truth."

They added a canonical-view rule to the schema while noting that independent copies could ignore it. The ledger could make correct consumption testable. It could not make every consumer correct.

The ledger did not prove the model was honest. It proved which exact model package China claimed to run, which exact evidence Singapore admitted, which query was asked, which output appeared, and which authorities failed when challenged.

"Remove process start time," China's technical objection said. "Combined with release measurement sequence, it exposes deployment cadence."

Rui replaced the raw start event with a commitment timestamp witnessed after the environment sealed and before evidence ingress.

"Remove credential identifiers."

She retained credential classes, validity windows, and public hashes; private identifiers stayed inside Chinese custody.

"Remove physical power video hash."

She replaced it with two signed observations: Chinese operator reported process stopped; Singapore operator observed cabinet power or session state under the agreed camera. Each statement remained limited to what its signer could see.

"The ledger permits false statements," Mara said from the other side of the table.

"All ledgers do. This one makes the speaker and scope explicit."

"China can sign that a process stopped while another continues."

"Yes."

"Singapore can sign what a camera shows while the camera omits another cabinet."

"Yes."

"Then why will providers accept it?"

"Because their alternative is accepting the same claims without a common sequence or named witness."

Mara read the record types again. "The part that leaves is not the verifier."

"No. It is the evidence of its boundary."

"Which China can refuse to sign."

"Refusal becomes a record."

"Signed by Singapore."

"And visible to every participant."

At 03:11, the emergency docket reviewed the schema. Lattice supported external replication if every provider received identical bytes. Tessera wanted raw process video. Cordant wanted input-commitment confidentiality. Vela wanted a right to append privacy objections without reopening past receipts. Orison wanted storage costs assigned to states.

China rejected replication.

Storage cost had become the first practical dispute. Replicating every participant record indefinitely would be trivial for a provider and material for an island regulator whose national archive shared bandwidth with weather services. Orison offered hosting, which would return the artifact to company custody. China offered observer access, which would return it to national custody. Singapore offered seven years of retention and no guarantee after.

The small states proposed tiered replication: the public chain and all signatures had to remain compact enough for any participant; sealed payloads stayed with lawful custodians; large evidence objects used redundant content-addressed archives funded by the parties whose claims depended on them. If an object disappeared, the ledger would preserve the missing commitment and custodian.

No one could turn retention into proof of permanence. They could make disappearance attributable.

"The ledger is an operational audit artifact of a national release," Liu said. "International observers may inspect it in Chinese custody and retain act-specific receipts. They may not reproduce the joined structure."

"Every record already exists outside China," Rui said.

"Not in this arrangement."

"Arrangement is not protected model material."

"It reveals which observations combine to establish confidence in national operation."

"That is the service being offered."

"Under Chinese governance."

The circle had become exact enough to see.

The Singapore chair asked whether the ledger could be produced from public and participant-owned receipts without China's protected records.

Rui said yes. China said any such artifact would be unofficial and incomplete.

"Then append that dispute," Mara said.

China objected to her phrasing. Neutral operations appended both statements.

At 03:28, Rui built a trial ledger from the first medical identity query. The release commitment hash came from the docket. Credential classes came from the witnessed manifest. Singapore supplied ingress and output-recognition receipts. Vela supplied its input commitment. China had already distributed the act-specific output and expiry result.

No protected file moved.

The assembled sequence matched every party's individual receipt.

China refused to sign the joined root.

The ledger appended:

```text DISPUTE — CHINA INDIVIDUAL RECORDS ARE AUTHENTIC. JOINED ARRANGEMENT IS NOT AN AUTHORIZED NATIONAL AUDIT ARTIFACT.

OBSERVATION — SINGAPORE THE RECORD HASHES AND ORDER MATCH WITNESSED DOCKET RECEIPTS. NO CLAIM ABOUT UNRECORDED CHINESE OPERATION IS MADE. ```

Lattice accepted the trial for stand-down use. Tessera accepted with a reservation. Cordant and Vela accepted after confidential input commitments remained hashes. Orison asked whether a future court could compel the underlying records. Singapore answered that existing law and custody terms applied; the ledger created no new immunity.

At 03:52, four governments accepted.

At 04:03, China withdrew Rui's remaining access to delegation-originated receipt verification. Her witness terminal could still read material already in neutral custody. It could no longer submit a hash to the Chinese delegation and receive confirmation that the source receipt matched its internal copy.

The border around the receipt window turned white.

"You expected this," Mara said.

"I expected refusal. Not the exact interface."

"Does it stop the ledger?"

Rui checked the remaining sources. Future windows would still publish act-specific receipts under the temporary service agreement. Singapore could bind its own witnessed sequence. Submitters could bind inputs. China could withhold a joined signature but could not withdraw the already promised output receipts without ending the service publicly.

"No," she said. "It makes China's nonparticipation visible."

"And if Liu stops issuing individual receipts?"

"Then the verifier stops."

"Which gives China veto."

"China operates the model. It always had veto. The ledger stops that veto from looking like technical impossibility."

Mara sat with the answer.

The next query window opened at 04:19. It examined two Lattice replacement credentials and Cordant's first capacity return. China issued the normal release commitment. Singapore opened ingress. Inputs arrived. The output reported one Lattice replacement valid, one missing a national payment exception, and Cordant's return incomplete because an Orison option remained exercisable.

At expiry, China issued the act-specific receipt. It did not sign the joined ledger.

Singapore appended the receipt hash and its observation. Cordant signed the incomplete result. Lattice signed the replacement states. The ledger root advanced without a Chinese joined signature.

"Can anyone alter it?" Mara asked.

"Anyone can create a different ledger," Rui said. "They cannot make the participants' signatures match altered records."

"Can China call it counterfeit?"

"It already calls it unauthorized."

"Will governments care?"

"They care that the receipts verify."

"Until China changes the receipt format."

Rui looked at the service agreement. China could propose a format change per window. Neutral recognition required participants to accept it before ingress.

"Then the ledger needs a format-change record," she said.

They added one.

At 04:47, Liu requested a final private channel.

"You are constructing a competing governance artifact from Chinese outputs," she said.

"I am joining receipts the service gives participants."

"You know which omissions matter because of protected experience."

"Then name the protected inference."

"The sequence of release, credential, ingress, query, output, and expiry reflects national deployment design."

"It reflects the public conditions I entered at 08:27."

"Conditions you derived from the system."

"From verification requirements."

"You cannot separate what you know from where you learned to know it."

Rui had no technical answer. Expertise always carried sources a receipt could not enumerate. States and companies used that ambiguity to claim ownership over a person's ability to recognize a problem.

"Then I will state my conflict," she said. "I designed parts of the national integration and use that experience to select ledger fields. The fields themselves bind only public and participant-owned receipts."

"That does not cure protected derivation."

"No. It makes the claim contestable."

"You are choosing foreign contest over national review."

"I requested review. You asked for seventy-two hours."

"Because proper review takes time."

"That is sometimes why being behind is safer. It is also how authority makes a deadline decide for it."

Liu's face hardened. "You will not use the quarantine policy as a public metaphor."

Rui understood that the exact interval was now closer to exposure than any manifest field.

"The ledger does not include it," she said.

"Your argument does."

"My argument belongs to me."

"Not when it reveals national policy."

"The policy is registered."

"Its operational consequence is protected."

They had reached the place where public fact became secret through context.

Liu ended the channel.

At 05:06, China filed an amended return request alleging imminent unauthorized disclosure of protected release practice. Singapore scheduled review after the compact deadline, preserving the earlier evidence window.

At 05:14, the neutral chair adopted the external ledger as an optional stand-down artifact. It would not replace Chinese receipts. It would join them with other participants' records and disputes. Acceptance required exact byte replication to every party. No record could be deleted; corrections appended.

Optional did not mean ignorable. Any party could refuse to recognize the joined artifact, as China did, but could not demand removal of its authentic individual receipts from copies lawfully held by recipients. Any government could rely on it for its own mandate and state exactly which records it accepted. Providers could challenge without joining. The design preserved fragmentation inside a common sequence.

Mara thought of the early compact promise that one neutral finding would reverse effects. The external ledger promised less. It offered no single finding, no universal reversal, and no custodian above politics. It gave disagreements addresses.

That modesty was why the parties could carry it out of the room.

At 05:30, the second ledger root reached twelve governments and six providers.

China received the bytes and appended no signature.

The part that left Chinese custody did not contain the model, its memory, its code, or the evidence it had inspected.

It contained the sequence of claims by which everyone else could decide whether one bounded use had occurred as promised.

For the first time, refusing China's verifier and accepting China's model were no longer the only choices.

The world could accept a receipt and retain the right to remember exactly why it remained insufficient.

---

## Chapter 23: Twelve Minutes Again

At 05:37, Mara learned that Lattice intended to surrender before anyone could take its authority away.

Keene's executive notice entered neutral custody with a 09:00 execution time. Every precommitted credential whose replacement had passed would expire as agreed. Every credential whose replacement remained incomplete would move to a thirty-day operating contract written by Lattice and offered to states on identical terms. If a state refused, the credential would expire anyway unless neutral operations found immediate physical danger.

It was surrender shaped as an ultimatum.

States could accept Lattice as contracted operator or assume a service before the replacement was ready. Keene would no longer decide whether emergency authority ended. He would decide the last terms under which Lattice kept operating after it ended.

"He is trying to outrun national revocation," Salma Arun said through the coalition channel.

"Yes," Mara replied.

"Can we stop him?"

"You spent seventeen hours asking him to bind expiry."

"Not on his clock."

"That is why clocks are power."

Mara heard how much she sounded like Keene and looked away from the channel. Dawn had found Singapore without improving it. Across the harbor, navigation lamps extinguished one row at a time while the room kept its artificial midnight. Someone had placed breakfast cartons outside the evidence suites. The carton beside Mara's door had her revoked Lattice identity printed on the label because the caterer had copied yesterday's access list.

She turned the label facedown.

The surrender notice was written in the language of relinquishment, but every deadline inside it preserved a choice for the company. A hospital could accept ten days of Lattice operation or discover, at nine in the morning, which of its patient identities the replacement registry had misunderstood. A port could sign the standard contract or explain why a ship carrying unstable industrial coolant had been held outside its channel. The terms were identical because Keene understood that equal coercion looked like neutrality when rendered as a template.

Mara marked every sentence that converted an absent public capability into private consent. There were fourteen. She sent the annotated notice to Arun without commentary.

"We cannot build fourteen institutions before breakfast," Arun said.

"No. But you can refuse to call their absence agreement."

"The public will hear that as wordplay."

"The public is entitled to know which services remain because governments chose them and which remain because no replacement exists. Put both on the receipt."

Arun exhaled close to her microphone. Behind her, advisers argued in three languages over a fraud-review credential whose manual fallback had been tested only against domestic transfers. "If we name the gaps, markets will attack them."

"They already exist. Secrecy only lets Keene price them."

Arun did not answer. A minute later, the coalition drafting pane acquired a new field: **PUBLIC CAPACITY ABSENT AT TRANSFER**.

The coalition had validated sixty-three percent of listed replacement paths. Another twenty-one percent could function with temporary manual exceptions. Sixteen percent remained dependent on company services no state could reproduce before 09:00.

Mara asked for the list.

Hospital identity in five jurisdictions. Cross-border payment fraud review. Port hazardous-cargo classification. Grid restoration forecasts. Emergency translation. Each authority could expire without immediate collapse. Together they would force thousands of local decisions into manual queues.

"Delay execution," Arun said.

"On what basis?"

"The verifier is still disputed."

"That is Lattice's argument for preserving control."

"Then accelerate replacements."

"The Chinese service can validate receipts. It cannot build missing public capacity."

Arun was quiet.

Mara saw the choice Keene had created. Stop his surrender and admit the states were not ready. Permit it and accept Lattice's operating contracts because the services still depended on the company. He had converted accountability into proof of indispensability one last time.

"Accept the standard contracts under protest for seven days," Mara said. "Not thirty. Public renewal every twenty-four hours. States hold credentials. Lattice operates. No exclusivity, no automatic extension, complete conflict disclosure."

"Will he agree?"

"He wants the expiry to happen more than he wants thirty days."

"Why?"

"Once Lattice surrenders first, every rival refusal looks worse."

Keene was not giving up power because Mara persuaded him it was right. He was spending power where it purchased the company's survival.

She remembered an old Lattice acquisition meeting in which he had described divestiture as a way of choosing the form in which an asset survived. Everyone else had heard a finance lesson. Mara had heard a governance principle and admired its cleanliness. Now the principle was being applied to sovereignty. The company would abandon the title and retain the dependency, then invoice the new owner for keeping it alive.

Her authorship did not begin with the cascade. It lived in the ordinary sentences she had once helped make respectable.

At 06:02, the coalition sent the seven-day counteroffer.

Before Keene answered, five affected agencies rejected the number for different reasons. One hospital authority needed fourteen days to reconcile cross-border identities. A port state could assume classification in forty-eight hours but needed Lattice's insurer translations for nine. A payments regulator said seven days was excessive because every additional day preserved private access to fraud reports. The translation service could move immediately if its language specialists remained available under personal contracts.

The coalition had negotiated with Lattice as if states shared one readiness date. They did not even share a definition of replacement. For the hospital, replacement meant the new state registry returned the right patient. For the port, it meant the state held the credential while Lattice staff still interpreted private annotations. For payments, continued access itself was the authority being removed.

Mara changed the counteroffer from a single duration to service-specific maximums. Arun changed it back.

"Keene will exploit every exception," she said.

"Then publish every exception."

"Ministers need one term to approve."

"Services need the term they can survive."

They compromised on ten days as an outer limit with daily, service-specific renewal. The simple number remained in the political announcement. The ledger carried the uneven reality beneath it.

At 06:05, Keene accepted twenty-one days with daily public renewal. At 06:08, the coalition answered seven. At 06:11, he offered fourteen. They settled at ten, renewable by affected states and revocable for receipt failure. Lattice could terminate service for nonpayment only after a public seventy-two-hour notice—longer than the window that had broken the world.

The agreement solved operation, not verification.

Cordant and Tessera still argued that China's external ledger did not prove why release 7.4 lacked continuity authority. Without the reason, China could substitute another release or integrate the capability while preserving the same public fields. China answered that every window committed the release. The providers answered that commitment showed identity, not strategic durability.

Mara knew the missing fact.

One hundred and eighty-three days from verified external deployment. Forty days remained when Rui examined the budgets. A frozen release could not quietly acquire the new architecture tomorrow without violating a national policy visible through release cadence.

The fact did not prove China would obey. It made violation more observable and politically costly. It explained why the useful weakness was not a feature switched on for the summit.

Rui had disclosed it to Mara in a service stair under their private rule. The rule had ended. The knowledge had not become Mara's to publish merely because it would help.

The neutral protocol required notice, not consent.

Mara opened a disclosure notice.

```text INTENDED DISCLOSURE: EXACT CHINESE CAPABILITY-QUARANTINE INTERVAL AND CURRENT REMAINING ELIGIBILITY PERIOD AS MATERIAL CONTEXT FOR THE STAND-DOWN VERIFIER'S RELEASE DURABILITY.

RECIPIENTS: COMPACT DOCKET / EXTERNAL BOUNDARY LEDGER

AUTHORITY CLAIMED: MATERIAL WITNESS KNOWLEDGE RECEIVED DURING ACTIVE ATTRIBUTION. ```

She addressed it to Rui and neutral operations.

Then she did not send it.

Notice would make the disclosure procedurally valid after twelve minutes. It would not make the choice hers. The private alliance had ended to prevent either of them wielding secret authority over the other. Using its last protected fact now would preserve the same authority in Mara alone.

She changed the intended action.

```text INTENDED ACTION: REQUEST THAT WITNESS SHEN DECIDE WHETHER AND HOW TO PLACE THE QUARANTINE INTERVAL INTO THE RECORD. ABSENT HER ACTION, WITNESS VENN WILL NOT DISCLOSE THE INTERVAL. ```

She sent it at 06:23.
